Official tracker searches

Apache Jira uses votes, recent updates and a title-focused async window. PostgreSQL archive windows use relevance and date for async messages from the past year. MySQL Bugs uses relevance and updated dates for asynchronous reports. Each window collects up to 20 entries. These metrics stay separate from GitHub reactions; every entry needs applicability review.

Review queue

Heat uses comments, positive reactions and repository stars. Coverage order rotates domains and takes one issue per repository in each round. Search hits are never counted as CoroPerf successes.

Ranking method

4*ln(1+comments) + 2*ln(1+positive_reactions) + 0.5*ln(1+repository_stars)

Each repository keeps its discussion head and recent-update windows. When more results exist, one deeper discussion page rotates every two hours, within the first 1,000 searchable results. Resuming a run keeps its saved page plan. Live rankings can change, so this is not exhaustive coverage.
